This is not actionable, whatever its title says.\n   - **Wrong project:** the issue asks to change an *existing* function or\n     class by name, and `check_api.py` lists that name in `missing`. The\n     reporter is using another library (often one built on Rich). Names from\n     the standard library or the reporter's own code (e.g. asyncio's\n     `wait_for`) do not count; the name the request is about does.\n   - **Intended behaviour:** before labelling `bug`, check whether the\n     behaviour follows from documented options. Consequently you won't be able to log Rich renderables (Table, Tree, etc) by calling `logger.debug` or other similar method.\n34-\n35-You could use the [capture](https://rich.readthedocs.io/en/latest/console.html#capturing-output) API to convert the renderable to a string and log that. However I would advise against it.\n36-\n--\n98-See the docs on [console markup](https://rich.readthedocs.io/en/latest/markup.html) for how to do this.\n99-\n100-<a name=\"why-does-emoji-break-alignment-in-a-table-or-panel\"></a>\n101:## Why does emoji break alignment in a Table or Panel?\n102-\n103-Certain emoji take up double space within the terminal. Unfortunately, terminals don't always agree how wide a given character should be.\n104-\n105-Rich has no way of knowing how wide a character will be on any given terminal. This can break alignment in containers like Table and Panel, where Rich needs to know the width of the content.\n106-\n107-There are also *multiple codepoints* characters, such as country flags, and emoji modifiers, which produce wildly different results across terminal emulators.\n108-\n109-Fortunately, most characters will work just fine. But you may have to avoid using the emojis that break alignment. You will get good results if you stick to emoji released on or before version 9 of the Unicode database,\n110-\n111-<hr>\n112-\n113-Generated by [FAQtory](https://github.com/willmcgugan/faqtory)\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

See :ref:`appendix_box` for a list of available box styles.\n\nPanels will extend to the full width of the terminal.
